# DESIGN BRIEF — `lcaconfbus1kill` pass 1: what should `lca_pci_conf` do for a non-zero bus?

**VERDICT LINE FIRST**, then the body.

Tree under review: **`GXEMUL-SEC/src/`**. Ignore any other `src/` under the project root — three
pristine 2021 upstream copies live there and give line numbers off by hundreds.

## PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

A recent panel here had seven of nine seats confirm a premise the brief stated wrongly. **Check the
premises, not just the derivations.** I have already had to withdraw one characterisation of this
very fix (see "A correction of mine" below), so treat my proposal as a sketch, not a design.

---

# SETTLED PREMISES (inlined; you need not read files)

**1. The defect is MEASURED, rung 3.** `regress/lca_confbus_probe.py` on unmodified `alphabook1`:
a guest `stq` of `0x1` to `LCA_IOC_CONF` is **accepted** (`idata & ~1` is 0), and the *next* guest
`ldq` into the PCI config window ends the host.

    bus ctl      yes                    yes                    control read at ioc_conf==0 survived   ok
    bus ctlsig   0xffffffffffffffff     0xffffffffffffffff     empty-slot ID probe signature          ok
    bus kill     dead                   alive                  <- THE DEFECT                          FAIL
    bus attrib   yes                    yes                    EVIDENCE: TODO. Non-zero bus.          ok

**2. The code.** `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)`, verbatim, after `#511`:

```c
	reg = (relative_addr >> 5) & 0xfc;
	tag = (relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff;

	bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;

	if (bus == 0) {
		for (dev=0; dev<21; dev++)
			if (tag & (0x800U << dev))	/*  #511  */
				break;
		if (dev >= 21) {
			/*  fatal("[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\n");
			exit(1);  */
			dev = 0;
		}
	} else {
		fatal("TODO. Non-zero bus.\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	func = (tag >> 8) & 7;
	/*  Pass PCI accesses onto bus_pci:  */
	bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pci_data, bus, dev, func, reg);
	bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pci_data, ...);
```

**3. The file's own comment on tag layout**, quoted verbatim:

```
	 *  According to NetBSD's lca_pci.c, the address is composed like this:
	 *	addr = tag << 5 | (regoffset & ~0x03) << 5 | 0x3 << 3
	 *
	 *  On the primary bus (nr 0):
	 *	tag = (1 << (device + 11)) | (function << 8);
	 *  and on other busses, the tag is a normal:
	 *	tag = (bus << 16) | (device << 11) | (function << 8)
```

**4. `bus_pci` is bus-agnostic**, verified: `cur_bus` is a plain `int` (`bus_pci.h:84`), devices are
a **linked list** (`first_device`), and lookup is `dev->bus == pci_data->cur_bus` (`bus_pci.c:102`).
Nothing is sized for one bus and nothing asserts. An empty-slot **read** returns
`0xffffffffffffffff` — which the probe's `bus ctlsig` row has MEASURED on bus 0.

**5. `fatal()` does not terminate**; only the explicit `exit(1)` does.

---

# A CORRECTION OF MINE, MADE BEFORE WRITING THIS

I earlier recorded that the fix is **"a DELETION — stop special-casing `bus != 0` and let the
existing path serve the cycle."** *** THAT IS WRONG AND I WITHDRAW IT. *** The `bus == 0` branch does
not merely guard — it **computes `dev`** from the IDSEL bit. Deleting the `else` would leave `dev`
**uninitialised** on the non-zero-bus path and pass garbage to `bus_pci_setaddr`.

So this is option-set **member 7 (IMPLEMENT)**, not a deletion. That changes what the round owes.

# THE SKETCH I WANT ATTACKED

Per premise 3, a non-zero-bus tag is `(bus << 16) | (device << 11) | (function << 8)`, so:

```c
	} else {
		dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;	/*  type-1: device is bits 15..11  */
	}
```

leaving `func` and `reg` as they already are, and letting `bus_pci_setaddr(bus, dev, func, reg)`
serve the cycle — an absent device on an absent bus then answers all-ones by itself.

## Questions

1. **Is `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` right?** The comment says device occupies bits 15..11 of the tag
   for type-1. **But `tag` here is `(relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff`, which has already been shifted** —
   so is the comment describing the same `tag` this code computes, or the pre-shift value? **This is
   the premise I am least sure of and it is the whole fix.**

2. **Where does `bus` come from architecturally?** The code takes it from `ioc_conf & 1` — a
   *register bit* — while the type-1 tag layout says the bus number is in **bits 23..16 of the tag**.
   Those are two different sources. **Which one does real LCA hardware use, and does the model's
   choice make the type-1 decode incoherent?** If `bus` should come from the tag, the fix is
   different and larger.

3. **Is IMPLEMENT even the right member here?** The alternatives: DECLINE the cycle silently;
   DECLINE with a latched announce; or return all-ones explicitly without touching `bus_pci`. Note
   that on real hardware a config read of an absent device returns all-ones, so declining and
   implementing may be **observationally identical for an empty bus** — and differ only if a device
   is ever placed on bus 1, which nothing in this tree does.

4. *** THE DISCRIMINATING ROW. *** The existing witness only checks that the host survives, and
   "survives" is true for a silent return, a latched announce, and a working implementation alike.
   I propose adding the mirror of `bus ctlsig` on the armed side: **the armed read must return
   `0xffffffffffffffff`**, which separates "serviced an empty bus" from "returned without
   servicing" (`0x0`, indistinguishable from unmapped). **Is that sufficient, or does it still
   conflate members?**

5. **What does this NOT close?** The `default:` arm of `lca_ioc` still exits for reads and writes,
   and `lca_interrupt_assert`/`_deassert` are still bare kills (filed as `lcairqstubkill`, witnessed).

6. If, while checking this, you found something ELSE — say so.

## Rules

* Answer from the source; name file and function for every behaviour you assert.
* Give the denominator: how many callers/consumers of `bus_pci_setaddr` you examined.
* A clean "your Q1 shift is wrong because X" is the most useful answer you can give.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:89: DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:90: {
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:91: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:92: 	int tag, bus, dev, func, reg;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:93: 	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) extra;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:94: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:95: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:96: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:97: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:98: 	/*
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:99: 	 *  1. Decompose the address into a tag.
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:100: 	 *
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:101: 	 *  According to NetBSD's lca_pci.c, the address is composed like this:
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:102: 	 *
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:103: 	 *	addr = tag << 5 | (regoffset & ~0x03) << 5 | 0x3 << 3
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:104: 	 */
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:105: 	reg = (relative_addr >> 5) & 0xfc;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:106: 	tag = (relative_addr >> 5) & ~0xff;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:107: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:108: 	/*
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:109: 	 *  2. Decompose the tag into bus, dev, and func.
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:110: 	 *
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:111: 	 *  The tag can be constructed in one of two ways. On the primary
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:112: 	 *  bus (nr 0):
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:113: 	 *
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:114: 	 *	tag = (1 << (device + 11)) | (function << 8);
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:115: 	 *
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:116: 	 *  and on other busses, the tag is a normal:
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:117: 	 *
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:118: 	 *	tag = (bus << 16) | (device << 11) | (function << 8)
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:119: 	 */
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:120: 	/*  printf("tag = 0x%x\n", (int)tag);  */
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:121: 	bus = d->ioc_conf & 1;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:122: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:123: 	if (bus == 0) {
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:124: 		/*  #511: was `0x800 << dev'.  `0x800' is a plain int (2^11) and `dev'
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:125: 		    reaches 20, so the last iteration evaluates 2^31 -- a SIGNED
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:126: 		    OVERFLOW, undefined by C99 6.5.7p4, not merely
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:127: 		    implementation-defined.  MEASURED under UBSAN before the change:
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:128: 		    "dev_lca.c:125:21: runtime error: left shift of 2048 by 20 places
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:129: 		    cannot be represented in type 'int'", reached by an ordinary guest
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:130: 		    ldq into the PCI config window (regress/lca_confbus_probe.py against
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:131: 		    build-asan; artifacts/lcashiftub/ubsan_HEAD_20260907.raw).
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:132: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:133: 		    `0x800U' makes the operand unsigned, so 2048U << 20 is 0x80000000U --
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:134: 		    inside unsigned int -- and NOTHING ELSE MOVES.
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:135: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:136: 		    *** THE REASON, CORRECTED: AN EARLIER DRAFT SAID `tag' IS "ALREADY A
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:137: 		    WIDER UNSIGNED QUANTITY".  THAT IS FALSE -- `tag' is a plain signed
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:138: 		    `int' (:92) -- AND FOUR PASS-2 SEATS CAUGHT IT.  Worse, the claim was
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:139: 		    backwards: being WIDER is what would CREATE a difference, not remove
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:140: 		    one.  Had `tag' been `uint64_t', the old `int' 0x80000000 is NEGATIVE
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:141: 		    and would SIGN-EXTEND to 0xffffffff80000000 for the `&', while
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:142: 		    `0x800U << 20' zero-extends to 0x0000000080000000 -- two different
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:143: 		    masks.  The seats were right about the mechanism and it does not fire
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:144: 		    here only because of the type.
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:145: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:146: 		    WHAT ACTUALLY HOLDS: both operands are 32-bit, so the `&' is performed
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:147: 		    at 32 bits either way -- `int & int' before, and after, `tag' converted
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:148: 		    to `unsigned int' by the usual arithmetic conversions.  The RESULT'S
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:149: 		    BIT PATTERN IS IDENTICAL; only its type changes, and it is consumed
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:150: 		    solely as a truth test in `if (...)'.  So no guest-visible value moves,
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:151: 		    and the repair is owed purely by the LANGUAGE. ***
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:152: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:153: 		    *** THE LOOP THIS SITS IN IS THIS FILE'S OWN GRACEFUL-DEGRADATION
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:154: 		    PRECEDENT -- the `dev >= 21' arm below has a commented-out
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:155: 		    fatal()/exit(1) and substitutes `dev = 0' instead, and `lcabadval'
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:156: 		    cites it as a transferable warrant.  The safe path contained the UB. ***  */
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:157: 		for (dev=0; dev<21; dev++)
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:158: 			if (tag & (0x800U << dev))	/*  #511  */
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:159: 				break;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:160: 		if (dev >= 21) {
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:161: 			/*  fatal("[ LCA: No bus 0 device? TODO ]\n");
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:162: 			exit(1);  */
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:163: 			dev = 0;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:164: 		}
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:165: 	} else {
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:166: 		fatal("TODO. Non-zero bus.\n");
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:167: 		exit(1);
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:168: 	}
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:169: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:170: 	func = (tag >> 8) & 7;
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:171: 	/*  printf("bus=%i dev=%i func=%i reg=%i\n", bus,dev,func,reg);  */
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:172: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:173: 	/*  Pass PCI accesses onto bus_pci:  */
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:174: 	bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pci_data, bus, dev, func, reg);
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:175: 	bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pci_data, writeflag == MEM_READ?
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:176: 	    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:177: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:178: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:179: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:180: 
GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:181: 	return 1;

The proposed device shift matches the local address construction. I also found a narrower contract in `bus_pci_data_access`: an absent-device read returns all-ones only at register 0; other registers return zero. I’m checking the hardware definition of `IOC_CONF` to settle the bus-number question.

web search: "lca_pci.c" "REGVAL(LCA_IOC_CONF)" NetBSD ...
codex
The hardware manual settles Q2: `IOC_CONF` supplies the configuration cycle type; the target bus number comes from the address. The sketch therefore needs a bus decode as well as the device decode. It also overstates the hardware absence behavior: LCA reports a master abort with error status, so the emulator’s all-ones ID result is a compatibility behavior.
web search:

274: /*
275:  *  allocate_device_space():
276:  *
277:  *  Used by glue code (see below) to allocate space for a PCI device.
278:  *
279:  *  The returned values in portp and memp are the actual (emulated) addresses
280:  *  that the device should use. (Normally only one of these is actually used.)
281:  *
282:  *  TODO: PCI irqs?
283:  */
284: static void allocate_device_space(struct pci_device *pd,
285: 	uint64_t portsize, uint64_t memsize,
286: 	uint64_t *portp, uint64_t *memp)
287: {
288: 	uint64_t port, mem;
289: 
290: 	/*  Calculate an aligned starting port:  */
291: 	port = pd->pcibus->cur_pci_portbase;
292: 	if (portsize != 0) {
293: 		port = ((port - 1) | (portsize - 1)) + 1;
294: 		pd->pcibus->cur_pci_portbase = port;
295: 		PCI_SET_DATA(PCI_MAPREG_START + pd->cur_mapreg_offset,
296: 		    port | PCI_MAPREG_TYPE_IO);
297: 		PCI_SET_DATA_SIZE(PCI_MAPREG_START + pd->cur_mapreg_offset,
298: 		    ((portsize - 1) & ~0xf) | 0xd);
299: 		pd->cur_mapreg_offset += sizeof(uint32_t);
300: 	}
301: 
302: 	/*  Calculate an aligned starting memory location:  */
303: 	mem = pd->pcibus->cur_pci_membase;
304: 	if (memsize != 0) {
305: 		mem = ((mem - 1) | (memsize - 1)) + 1;
306: 		pd->pcibus->cur_pci_membase = mem;
307: 		PCI_SET_DATA(PCI_MAPREG_START + pd->cur_mapreg_offset, mem);
308: 		PCI_SET_DATA_SIZE(PCI_MAPREG_START + pd->cur_mapreg_offset,
309: 		    ((memsize - 1) & ~0xf) | 0x0);
310: 		pd->cur_mapreg_offset += sizeof(uint32_t);
311: 	}
312: 
313: 	*portp = port + pd->pcibus->pci_actual_io_offset;
314: 	*memp  = mem  + pd->pcibus->pci_actual_mem_offset;
315: 
316: 	if (verbose >= 2) {
317: 		debug("pci device '%s' at", pd->name);
318: 		if (portsize != 0)
319: 			debug(" port 0x%llx-0x%llx", (long long)pd->pcibus->
320: 			    cur_pci_portbase, (long long)(pd->pcibus->
321: 			    cur_pci_portbase + portsize - 1));
322: 		if (memsize != 0)
323: 			debug(" mem 0x%llx-0x%llx", (long long)pd->pcibus->
324: 			    cur_pci_membase, (long long)(pd->pcibus->
325: 			    cur_pci_membase + memsize - 1));
326: 		debug("\n");
327: 	}
328: 
329: 	pd->pcibus->cur_pci_portbase += portsize;
330: 	pd->pcibus->cur_pci_membase += memsize;
331: }

394: void debugmsg_set_verbosity_level(int subsystem, int verbosity)
395: {
396: 	if (subsystem != SUBSYS_ALL)
397: 		debugmsg_current_verbosity[subsystem] = verbosity;
398: 	else
399: 		for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
400: 			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] = verbosity;
401: }
402: 
403: 
404: void debugmsg_add_verbosity_level(int subsystem, int verbosity_delta)
405: {
406: 	if (subsystem != SUBSYS_ALL)
407: 		debugmsg_current_verbosity[subsystem] += verbosity_delta;
408: 	else
409: 		for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
410: 			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] += verbosity_delta;
411: 
412: 	for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
413: 		if (debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] < 0)
414: 			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] = 0;
415: }

423: int debugmsg_register_subsystem(const char* name)
424: {
425: 	// TODO: Another way would be to support multiple names with e.g.
426: 	// a suffix. Such as "le0", "le1", "le2" for the name "le".
427: 	// But for now, simply return the same subsystem for all of them.
428: 	ssize_t subsys = -1;
429: 	for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
430: 		if (strcmp(name, debugmsg_subsystem_name[i]) == 0)
431: 			subsys = i;
432: 
433: 	if (subsys >= 0)
434: 		return subsys;
435: 	
436: 	subsys = debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems;
437: 
438: 	debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems ++;
439: 	debugmsg_subsystem_name = realloc(debugmsg_subsystem_name,
440: 	    sizeof(char*) * debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems);
441: 	debugmsg_current_verbosity = realloc(debugmsg_current_verbosity,
442: 	    sizeof(int) * debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems);;

444: 	    sizeof(int) * debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems);

351: struct pci_data *bus_pci_init(struct machine *machine, const char *irq_path,
352: 	uint64_t pci_actual_io_offset, uint64_t pci_actual_mem_offset,
353: 	uint64_t pci_portbase, uint64_t pci_membase, const char *pci_irqbase,
354: 	uint64_t isa_portbase, uint64_t isa_membase, const char *isa_irqbase)
355: {
356: 	struct pci_data *d;
357: 
358: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct pci_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct pci_data)));
359: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct pci_data));
360: 
361: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d->irq_path     = strdup(irq_path));
362: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d->irq_path_isa = strdup(isa_irqbase));
363: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d->irq_path_pci = strdup(pci_irqbase));
364: 
365: 	d->pci_actual_io_offset  = pci_actual_io_offset;
366: 	d->pci_actual_mem_offset = pci_actual_mem_offset;
367: 	d->pci_portbase          = pci_portbase;
368: 	d->pci_membase           = pci_membase;
369: 	d->isa_portbase          = isa_portbase;
370: 	d->isa_membase           = isa_membase;
371: 
372: 	d->cur_pci_portbase = d->pci_portbase;
373: 	d->cur_pci_membase  = d->pci_membase;
374: 
375: 	/*  Assume that the first 64KB could be used by legacy ISA devices:  */
376: 	if (d->isa_portbase != 0 || d->isa_membase != 0) {
377: 		d->cur_pci_portbase += 0x10000;
378: 		d->cur_pci_membase  += 0x10000;
379: 	}
380: 
381: 	return d;
382: }

386: /******************************************************************************
387:  *                                                                            *
388:  *  The following is glue code for PCI controllers and devices. The glue      *
389:  *  code does the minimal stuff necessary to get an emulated OS to detect     *
390:  *  the device (i.e. set up PCI configuration registers), and then if         *
391:  *  necessary adds a "normal" device.                                         *
392:  *                                                                            *
393:  ******************************************************************************/

397: /*
398:  *  Integraphics Systems "igsfb" Framebuffer (graphics) card, used in at
399:  *  least the NetWinder.
400:  */
401: 
402: #define	PCI_VENDOR_INTEGRAPHICS		0x10ea
403: 
404: PCIINIT(igsfb)
405: {

web search: 'All other devices must ignore Type 1'
codex
The witness needs two qualifications: its config offset is zero, so it exercises type-1 mode with a zero bus field. Also, `printed()` takes the first register match; adding a second `print t2` without changing the parser would recheck the control value. A unit fixture with a distinct value on bus 2 would distinguish correct routing from the proposed bus-1-only routing and an explicit all-ones response.
codex
**VERDICT: IMPLEMENT, with revisions. Your device shift is correct. Type-1 must also decode the bus from the tag, and the armed all-ones row cannot prove implementation.**

1. **Q1: `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is correct.**

   In `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)`, the comment’s `tag` is the value **before address construction shifts it left**. The existing `relative_addr >> 5` reverses that construction; `& ~0xff` then removes the register-offset bits. The low `0x3 << 3` access encoding disappears during the right shift. Consequently, device bits are now **15..11**, exactly as your sketch assumes. Existing `func` and `reg` extraction remain correct for those fields. [dev_lca.c:98](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:98)

   Independent corroboration: the historical OpenBSD driver’s `lca_conf_read()` constructs the access with `tag << 5`; its `lca_decompose_tag()` extracts device with `>> 11`. [lca_pci.c:97](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_guest-src-openbsd22/sys/arch/alpha/pci/lca_pci.c:97)

2. **Q2: `IOC_CONF` selects the configuration format; it does not supply the target bus number.**

   Real LCA copies `IOC_CONF[1:0]` onto PCI `AD[1:0]`. Those encode type 0 (`00`) or type 1 (`01`). The target bus is a separate type-1 address field. [LCA hardware manual, §6.4.2](https://ftp.zx.net.nz/pub/archive/ftp.digital.com/pub/DEC/DECinfo/semiconductor/literature/21066hrm.pdf#page=228), [PCI specification, §3.2.2.3.1](https://lekensteyn.nl/files/docs/PCI_SPEV_V3_0.pdf#page=48)

   For ordinary nonzero-bus type-1 cycles, the replacement in `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)` therefore needs **both** assignments:

   ```c
   } else {
       bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;
       dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;
   }
   ```

   Your sketch otherwise routes every type-1 request to bus 1. Keep the **branch selection based on `ioc_conf`**; extracting the bus first and branching on that would misinterpret overlapping type-0 IDSEL bits. [dev_lca.c:121](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:121)

3. **Q3: IMPLEMENT is justified, but specify its limits.**

   `bus_pci_setaddr()` stores the supplied tuple, and `bus_pci_data_access()` matches the complete bus/device/function tuple against its device list. That supports implementing the decode without redesigning the backend. [bus_pci.c:192](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/bus_pci.c:192), [bus_pci.c:99](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/bus_pci.c:99)

   **Premise 4’s absence contract is overstated.** In `bus_pci_data_access()`, an absent target produces:

   | Operation | Existing behavior |
   |---|---|
   | Read at register 0 | `0xffffffffffffffff` |
   | Read at another register | Zero |
   | Write | Calls `fatal()` on every access, then returns |

   There is no diagnostic latch in that branch. `fatal()` itself returns. [bus_pci.c:109](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/bus_pci.c:109), [debugmsg.c:384](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c:384)

   Thus explicitly returning all-ones everywhere already differs from delegation on an empty bus. A deliberate absence response is a defensible containment choice, but its observable contract needs stating.

   **Also define type-1 requests encoding bus 0.** The two assignments above decode fields; they do not enforce routing. Forwarding such requests directly can reach primary-bus entries because the backend receives no cycle-type information. LCA already populates a primary-bus device in `DEVINIT(lca)`. I recommend treating type-1/bus-0 requests as absent in this model. PCI type-1 transactions are forwarded through bridges to another bus segment; ordinary local targets ignore them. [dev_lca.c:389](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:389), [PCI specification, p. 49](https://lekensteyn.nl/files/docs/PCI_SPEV_V3_0.pdf#page=49)

4. **Q4: Add the armed signature, but it remains an absence-result test.**

   It distinguishes all-ones from a successful return that leaves the read buffer untouched: `MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE()` clears that buffer before device dispatch. [memory_rw.c:485](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:485)

   It still conflates correct delegation, explicit all-ones, and incorrect decoding that happens to select another absent target. Diagnostic policy also needs a separate oracle.

   Two details in the existing witness matter:

   - Its top-level setup accesses **configuration offset zero**. In type-1 format that encodes **bus 0**, not bus 1. The supplied measurement proves the mode-selected termination defect, but does not validate secondary-bus routing. [lca_confbus_probe.py:167](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lca_confbus_probe.py:167)
   - `printed()` uses `re.search()`, selecting the **first** register print. Merely appending another `print t2` and calling the same parser would recheck the control result. Parse the armed result independently. [lca_confbus_probe.py:157](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lca_confbus_probe.py:157)

   **The missing discriminating measurement:** an isolated handler fixture with distinguishable configuration contents at the same device/function on buses 1 and 2. Verify that a bus-2 request returns bus-2 contents, with nonzero device, function, and register fields; verify write dispatch separately. That tests the `lca_pci_conf` → `bus_pci_setaddr` → `bus_pci_data_access` path against both canned absence and your sketch’s fixed-bus mistake.

5. **Q5: This closes only the selected configuration-path termination.**

   The following remain independent:

   - `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)` default exits for reads and writes. [dev_lca.c:351](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:351)
   - `lca_interrupt_assert()` and `lca_interrupt_deassert()` still exit. [dev_lca.c:70](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:70)
   - `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)` still exits for rejected `IOC_CONF` bits; `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)` has another explicit exit on its offset/length guard. [dev_lca.c:229](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:229), [dev_lca.c:193](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:193)

6. **Q6: The hardware all-ones premise also needs correction.**

   Real LCA’s master-abort behavior specifies arbitrary read data with error status, an instruction exception, and error reporting/logging—not a guaranteed successful all-ones load. [LCA hardware manual, §6.2.18](https://ftp.zx.net.nz/pub/archive/ftp.digital.com/pub/DEC/DECinfo/semiconductor/literature/21066hrm.pdf#page=211)

   The historical OpenBSD `lca_conf_read()` initializes its result to all-ones and uses `badaddr()` before reading. That distinction matters: the emulator’s measured ID signature is a compatibility contract, not evidence that full LCA absence/error semantics are implemented. [lca_pci.c:142](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_guest-src-openbsd22/sys/arch/alpha/pci/lca_pci.c:142)

**VERDICT: IMPLEMENT, with revisions. Your device shift is correct. Type-1 must also decode the bus from the tag, and the armed all-ones row cannot prove implementation.**

1. **Q1: `dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f` is correct.**

   In `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)`, the comment’s `tag` is the value **before address construction shifts it left**. The existing `relative_addr >> 5` reverses that construction; `& ~0xff` then removes the register-offset bits. The low `0x3 << 3` access encoding disappears during the right shift. Consequently, device bits are now **15..11**, exactly as your sketch assumes. Existing `func` and `reg` extraction remain correct for those fields. [dev_lca.c:98](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:98)

   Independent corroboration: the historical OpenBSD driver’s `lca_conf_read()` constructs the access with `tag << 5`; its `lca_decompose_tag()` extracts device with `>> 11`. [lca_pci.c:97](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_guest-src-openbsd22/sys/arch/alpha/pci/lca_pci.c:97)

2. **Q2: `IOC_CONF` selects the configuration format; it does not supply the target bus number.**

   Real LCA copies `IOC_CONF[1:0]` onto PCI `AD[1:0]`. Those encode type 0 (`00`) or type 1 (`01`). The target bus is a separate type-1 address field. [LCA hardware manual, §6.4.2](https://ftp.zx.net.nz/pub/archive/ftp.digital.com/pub/DEC/DECinfo/semiconductor/literature/21066hrm.pdf#page=228), [PCI specification, §3.2.2.3.1](https://lekensteyn.nl/files/docs/PCI_SPEV_V3_0.pdf#page=48)

   For ordinary nonzero-bus type-1 cycles, the replacement in `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_pci_conf)` therefore needs **both** assignments:

   ```c
   } else {
       bus = (tag >> 16) & 0xff;
       dev = (tag >> 11) & 0x1f;
   }
   ```

   Your sketch otherwise routes every type-1 request to bus 1. Keep the **branch selection based on `ioc_conf`**; extracting the bus first and branching on that would misinterpret overlapping type-0 IDSEL bits. [dev_lca.c:121](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:121)

3. **Q3: IMPLEMENT is justified, but specify its limits.**

   `bus_pci_setaddr()` stores the supplied tuple, and `bus_pci_data_access()` matches the complete bus/device/function tuple against its device list. That supports implementing the decode without redesigning the backend. [bus_pci.c:192](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/bus_pci.c:192), [bus_pci.c:99](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/bus_pci.c:99)

   **Premise 4’s absence contract is overstated.** In `bus_pci_data_access()`, an absent target produces:

   | Operation | Existing behavior |
   |---|---|
   | Read at register 0 | `0xffffffffffffffff` |
   | Read at another register | Zero |
   | Write | Calls `fatal()` on every access, then returns |

   There is no diagnostic latch in that branch. `fatal()` itself returns. [bus_pci.c:109](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/bus_pci.c:109), [debugmsg.c:384](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c:384)

   Thus explicitly returning all-ones everywhere already differs from delegation on an empty bus. A deliberate absence response is a defensible containment choice, but its observable contract needs stating.

   **Also define type-1 requests encoding bus 0.** The two assignments above decode fields; they do not enforce routing. Forwarding such requests directly can reach primary-bus entries because the backend receives no cycle-type information. LCA already populates a primary-bus device in `DEVINIT(lca)`. I recommend treating type-1/bus-0 requests as absent in this model. PCI type-1 transactions are forwarded through bridges to another bus segment; ordinary local targets ignore them. [dev_lca.c:389](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:389), [PCI specification, p. 49](https://lekensteyn.nl/files/docs/PCI_SPEV_V3_0.pdf#page=49)

4. **Q4: Add the armed signature, but it remains an absence-result test.**

   It distinguishes all-ones from a successful return that leaves the read buffer untouched: `MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE()` clears that buffer before device dispatch. [memory_rw.c:485](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:485)

   It still conflates correct delegation, explicit all-ones, and incorrect decoding that happens to select another absent target. Diagnostic policy also needs a separate oracle.

   Two details in the existing witness matter:

   - Its top-level setup accesses **configuration offset zero**. In type-1 format that encodes **bus 0**, not bus 1. The supplied measurement proves the mode-selected termination defect, but does not validate secondary-bus routing. [lca_confbus_probe.py:167](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lca_confbus_probe.py:167)
   - `printed()` uses `re.search()`, selecting the **first** register print. Merely appending another `print t2` and calling the same parser would recheck the control result. Parse the armed result independently. [lca_confbus_probe.py:157](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lca_confbus_probe.py:157)

   **The missing discriminating measurement:** an isolated handler fixture with distinguishable configuration contents at the same device/function on buses 1 and 2. Verify that a bus-2 request returns bus-2 contents, with nonzero device, function, and register fields; verify write dispatch separately. That tests the `lca_pci_conf` → `bus_pci_setaddr` → `bus_pci_data_access` path against both canned absence and your sketch’s fixed-bus mistake.

5. **Q5: This closes only the selected configuration-path termination.**

   The following remain independent:

   - `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)` default exits for reads and writes. [dev_lca.c:351](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:351)
   - `lca_interrupt_assert()` and `lca_interrupt_deassert()` still exit. [dev_lca.c:70](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:70)
   - `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)` still exits for rejected `IOC_CONF` bits; `DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)` has another explicit exit on its offset/length guard. [dev_lca.c:229](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:229), [dev_lca.c:193](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_lca.c:193)

   Real LCA’s master-abort behavior specifies arbitrary read data with error status, an instruction exception, and error reporting/logging—not a guaranteed successful all-ones load.    The historical OpenBSD `lca_conf_read()` initializes its result to all-ones and uses `badaddr()` before reading. That distinction matters: the emulator’s measured ID signature is a compatibility contract, not evidence that full LCA absence/error semantics are implemented.
